<span class="ct-node-content">
<span class="ct-node-label">SAP Secrecy</span>
<span class="ct-node-summary">Special-access-programs, usually shortened to SAPs, are not a separate level of classification above Top Secret. They are extra access, handling and oversight regimes placed around particularly sensitive classified information when ordinary Confidential, Secret or Top Secret controls are judged insufficient.</span>
</span>
</a>

<span class="ct-node-label">Need to Know</span>
<span class="ct-node-title-full">How Much Should a Subcontractor See?</span>
<span class="ct-node-summary">Prime contractors can split classified work so subcontractors see only the component, interface or tolerance needed for their task.</span>
</span>

<span class="ct-node-content">
<span class="ct-node-label">Waived SAPs</span>
<span class="ct-node-title-full">What Waived SAPs Really Hide</span>
<span class="ct-node-summary">Waived SAPs are not outside oversight, but they move the reporting path into a much smaller congressional channel.</span>
</span>
</a>

<span class="ct-node-label">Narrow Reporting</span>
<span class="ct-node-title-full">What gets lost when reporting narrows?</span>

</span>
